Or write the summary before the loop, where it cannot report what happened. | The requirements state the failure semantics exactly — log, skip the affected record, continue the run, do not halt the scheduled run — and an uncaught exception in one record's mapping breaks all four clauses at once. The `finally` matters independently: without it a run that fails late produces **no** summary, and the acceptance evidence for that run is then indistinguishable from a run that never started. | A run can now complete "successfully" while having skipped many records, so the summary's counters are the only signal — which is why the checklist requires non-zero `processed` and zero `skipped` rather than merely a completed run. | High | — |
| **D-238** | Give founder, executive, funding round and job posting **stable identity keys** and upsert on them, so a scheduled replay updates rather than duplicates: founder and executive on `startup` + `name`; funding round on `startup` + `round_date` + `round_type`, falling back to `startup` + `round_date`; job posting on `startup` + `url`, falling back to `startup` + `title` + `posted_date`. A provider-supplied identifier is not available for the fallback dataset, which must be hand-authorable. A post-hoc sweep would leave the data wrong between runs. | A natural key is a judgement about identity: two genuinely distinct rounds on the same date and type for one startup collapse into one, and two people of the same name at one company collapse into one. Or delete only the entity rows and leave the staged copies. | The operation could only live on a ninth Script Include, which the frozen inventory does not license — and the design work behind it is preserved rather than discarded, because it is the reason the absence is stated as a bounded, specific gap: an AND-combined unique match, deletion rather than blanking on tables whose `name` column is mandatory, and a second pass over `raw_payload`, which is the one column holding the verbatim upstream payload. Or log nothing beyond the identifier. | An unescaped value breaks the `key=\"value\"` log format and can forge a log line; an uncapped one can push a whole request body into the log. Resolving upward — fail-open at the highest severity — means a mis-typed severity produces a **noisy** log rather than a silent loss; resolving downward would hide the event exactly when something is already wrong. | An unrecognised severity is reported as an error, so a typo in a call site inflates the error count rather than being ignored. Having the rule read the derived column would make the cache its own input, so an error in the projection would become self-confirming. | A rename of the shared method breaks four call sites at once; it is on the documented rename-impact list. | Low | — |

Amends D-010. | Let the projection write re-enter business rules, which is the platform default. Or store the list truncated to the column length. An emptied projection plus an error line is unmistakable, and the join table still holds the complete record. | **D-246** | Mint the ATF REST caller as a **disposable, web-service-access-only identity created and destroyed inside the test transaction**, keeping exactly one credential-less shell record for the build-time reference the inbound step requires. Or skip the REST assertions that need a caller. | A persistent user with a password, interactive access and no expiry is a standing credential created by a test — and it sits on `sys_user`, outside the application scope, which the requirements forbid modifying. Interactive access is denied and no role is granted, which bounds what a survivor could do, but it is not a credential-less record and must not be described as one. Three mechanisms close the window rather than one: the suite's first test sweeps any identity a previous run left behind before it mints its own, every mint is paired with a retirement in a `finally` block, and four post-suite residue queries make a survivor detectable rather than merely unlikely. The requirement is stated at **D-292**. Rotation of the one credential-less shell record is documented separately. | High | `AUTHZ` |

Amends D-063. | Call the mapper directly, as delivered originally, which is simpler and needs no flow. Or start the flow in the background. Or assert the flow by inspecting its definition rather than running it. | Calling the mapper tests the mapper; it cannot observe the cadence guard, the branch taken, the credential resolution, the Log action or the provenance write, all of which live in the flow. Two runs per test are what make the guard assertable on both branches. A background start runs in another transaction, so the next step reads nothing **and** the writes escape ATF's rollback — the one variant that both fails to assert and leaves debris. Falling back to a direct call would let a green result mean either "the flow works" or "the flow never ran". | A foreground flow run inside a test is slower and depends on the Flow API being available on the instance. Accepted: an unavailable API now fails loudly, which is the correct signal. A builder chain that never reaches `.run()` constructs no execution while still looking like a start, so the flow is named by its `internal_name`, the identifier is read from what `.run()` returned, and both are asserted — the requirement is at **D-291**. | High | — |

Or assert against a total read from a second query, which restates the implementation. | A greater-than-or-equal assertion passes against almost any wrong total, including a capped one — which is how the capped member of D-041 survived its own test suite. A known cardinality with an isolating filter is the only form that can fail. One statement with three named gates makes the question answerable: which gate is open, and what does that block. | **D-250** | Make the review document's secret check **value-aware** — a credential-shaped name bound to a long opaque literal — rather than a zero-hit count of placeholder words. Worse, it would pass a real secret whose name is not on the word list. A value-shaped test inverts both properties. | A value-aware pattern can still miss a short or unusually-shaped secret. Or renumber the whole file so the new decisions sit in their thematic sections. | Sibling documents and the Rule 3 review cite `D-###` identifiers directly, so renumbering would invalidate citations across four files at once — and this file's own reading rules promise identifiers are never reused or renumbered. Rewriting in place would erase the fact that a decision **changed**, which is precisely what a reader auditing a remediation needs to see. | **D-252** | Extend the flag inventory from twelve entries to **seventeen**, and later to nineteen at **D-300**, and introduce a **fifth** disposition, **partially implemented, flagged**, for a requirement met in part whose shortfall is specific, bounded and observable. None of the three existing dispositions fits a requirement that is genuinely met in part: *flagged, not implemented* overstates the shortfall, *resolved by workaround* understates it, and *consciously excluded* is simply false. The requirements forbid **silently** half-implementing a requirement, not half-implementing one, so a fourth disposition is how the prohibition is honoured rather than evaded. | A reader who counts flags will find nineteen where the plan named ten and earlier revisions named twelve and seventeen.
